**6.115.10**  Then all the wives of Daśaratha mounted their vehicles and, placing Kausalyā and Sumitrā at the fore, went forth.

**6.115.11**  With the sound of the horses' hooves, the rumble of chariot wheels, and the blare of conches and kettledrums, the earth seemed to tremble.

**6.115.12**  The entire city came to Nandigrāma: the pious Bharata, along with the foremost brāhmaṇas, the heads of guilds, and the merchant-leaders.

**6.115.13**  Bharata, surrounded by ministers holding garlands and sweets in their hands, was honored with the sounds of conches and kettledrums and the praises of the bards.

**6.115.14**  That knower of righteousness, having taken up the sandals of his noble elder brother upon his head, bore the white parasol adorned with white garlands,

**6.115.15**  and the two white yak-tail fans worthy of a king, ornamented with gold — Bharata himself emaciated from fasting, wretched, clad in bark and black antelope skin.

**6.115.16**  Having heard of his brother's return, and overcome with prior joy, the great-souled Bharata then went forward to meet Rāma, together with his ministers.

**6.115.17**  Gazing about, Bharata spoke to the son of the wind: "Surely the fickleness of the monkey race has not prevailed? For I do not yet see noble Rāma of Kākutstha's line, the scorcher of foes."

**6.115.18**  When these words had been spoken, Hanūmān said the following, imparting the news to the truly valorous Bharata:

**6.115.19**  "By the grace of Bharadvāja, he has reached trees always laden with fruit and flowers, honey-dripping, resounding with intoxicated bees —

**6.115.20**  O scorcher of foes, and this boon was granted by Vāsava himself: hospitality was extended to him together with his army, complete in every virtue.

**6.115.21**  A great clamor is heard from the exultant forest-dwellers; I think that monkey army is crossing the river Gomatī.

**6.115.22**  See the shower of dust arising toward the sandy riverbank; I think the leapers are shaking the pleasant grove of sāla trees.

**6.115.23**  That which is seen in the distance, pure and resembling the moon — that is the divine Puṣpaka aerial car, created by Brahmā through the power of his mind.

**6.115.24**  After slaying Rāvaṇa along with his kinsmen, this divine, mind-swift vehicle was obtained by the great-souled one by the favor of Kubera, the lord of wealth.

**6.115.25**  In it are those two heroic brothers of the Rāghu line together with Vaidehī, and the great-splendored Sugrīva, and Vibhīṣaṇa, the lord of the Rākṣasas."

**6.115.26**  Then a joyful clamor, arising from women, children, youths and elders alike, rose up to heaven: "This is Rāma!" they proclaimed.

**6.115.27**  Descending from chariots, elephants, and horses and reaching the ground, those men beheld him standing in the aerial car, like the moon in the sky.

**6.115.28**  Bharata, with joined palms, filled with joy, his face turned toward Rāghava, then honored Rāma with a welcome that was wholly fitting.

**6.115.29**  Lakṣmaṇa's elder brother, with his broad and long eyes, shone in the aerial car created by Brahmā's mind, like a second Indra, the bearer of the thunderbolt.

**6.115.30**  Then Bharata, bowing down, reverenced his brother Rāma positioned at the summit of the aerial car, as one would revere the sun standing upon Mount Meru.

**6.115.31**  Lifted up into the aerial car, the truly valorous Bharata, having reached Rāma, joyfully saluted him once again.

**6.115.32**  Raising him up, the descendant of Kākutstha, delighted, placed on his lap Bharata, who had for so long been out of sight, and embraced him.

**6.115.33**  Then the scorcher of foes Bharata, going to Lakṣmaṇa and to Vaidehī, saluted them with affection and addressed them by name.

**6.115.34**  The son of Kaikeyī embraced Sugrīva, Jāmbavān, and Aṅgada, and Mainda, Dvivida, Nīla, and Ṛṣabha as well.

**6.115.35**  Those shape-shifting monkeys, taking on human form, joyfully enquired after Bharata's well-being.

**6.115.36**  Reassuring Vibhīṣaṇa, Bharata spoke: "By good fortune was this extremely difficult deed accomplished with you as ally."

**6.115.37**  Then Śatrughna, saluting Rāma together with Lakṣmaṇa, afterward reverenced the feet of Sītā with great humility.

**6.115.38**  Rāma, approaching his mother, who was dejected and wasted by grief, bowed and clasped her feet, gladdening her heart.

**6.115.39**  Having saluted Sumitrā, the illustrious Kaikeyī, and all his mothers, he then went to the family priest.

**6.115.40**  "Welcome to you, O mighty-armed one, increaser of Kausalyā's joy" — thus all the townspeople, with joined palms, said to Rāma.

**6.115.41**  Those thousands of joined palms upraised by the townspeople were beheld by Bharata's elder brother like lotuses that had opened their buds.

**6.115.42**  Then Bharata himself, the knower of righteousness, taking Rāma's sandals, placed them upon the feet of that lord of men.

**6.115.43**  Then Bharata, with joined palms, said to Rāma: "O king, this kingdom, which was entrusted to me, I now return to you."

**6.115.44**  "Today my birth is fulfilled and my heart's desire accomplished, in that I behold you, my king, returned to Ayodhyā once more."

**6.115.45**  "May you review the treasury, the granaries, the city, and the army — everything has been increased tenfold by your glory through me."

**6.115.46**  Seeing Bharata speaking thus, so devoted to his brother, the monkeys shed tears, as did the Rākṣasa Vibhīṣaṇa.

**6.115.47**  Then, in great joy, Rāghava placed Bharata upon his lap and proceeded with that aerial car, along with his army, to Bharata's hermitage.

**6.115.48**  Having reached Bharata's hermitage, Rāghava then, together with his army, descended from the summit of the aerial car and stood upon the earth.

**6.115.49**  Then Rāma addressed that peerless aerial car: "Carry the god Vaiśravaṇa. I give you leave — depart."

**6.115.50**  Then that peerless aerial car, dismissed by Rāma, went toward the northern quarter, to the home of Kubera, the lord of wealth.

**6.115.51**  The mighty Rāghava, like Indra the lord of the immortals with Bṛhaspati, pressed the feet of the family priest who was equal to his own self, and then sat down together with him on an auspicious seat.

**6.116.1**  Placing his joined palms upon his head, Bharata, the increaser of Kaikeyī's joy, addressed his elder brother Rāma of true valor:

**6.116.2**  "My mother was honored, and this kingdom was given to me; I now restore it to you just as you placed it in my hands."

**6.116.3**  "The yoke laid down by a single mighty bull — I, like a young calf, am not able to bear this heavy burden."

**6.116.4**  "Like a dam broken by the great force of water and leaking, I consider this breach in the kingdom, left unclosed, difficult to stop."

**6.116.5**  "As a donkey cannot follow the gait of a horse, nor a crow that of a goose, so I, O lord, O subduer of enemies, am not able to follow your path."

**6.116.6**  "Just as a tree planted and grown within a courtyard becomes great, very difficult to climb, with a massive trunk and spreading branches —

**6.116.7**  "— if it withers after flowering without displaying its fruits, then the purpose of the one for whose sake it was planted is not fulfilled:

**6.116.8**  "This is the analogy, O mighty-armed one; you should understand its meaning. O lord of men, if you do not govern us, your devoted servants, the purpose fails."

**6.116.9**  "Let the world today behold you, anointed king, resplendent on all sides like the sun blazing at midday with brilliant light."

**6.116.10**  "May you awake and sleep to the combined sounds of musical instruments, to the jingling of girdles and anklets, and to the sweet sounds of song."

**6.116.11**  "For as long as the wheel of time turns, and for as long as this earth endures, so long may you exercise here lordship over all."

**6.116.12**  Hearing Bharata's words, Rāma, the conqueror of enemy cities, accepted with "So be it" and sat down on the auspicious seat.

**6.116.13**  Then, at Śatrughna's command, skilled barbers with gentle hands, swift and deft, attended upon Rāghava.

**6.116.14**  First Bharata bathed, then the mighty Lakṣmaṇa, then Sugrīva the lord of monkeys, and then Vibhīṣaṇa the lord of Rākṣasas —

**6.116.15**  Rāma, with his matted locks removed, bathed, anointed with colorful garlands, adorned with the most excellent garments, stood there blazing with royal glory.

**6.116.16**  The glorious increaser of the Ikṣvāku line caused the adornment of Rāma, and also of Lakṣmaṇa, to be performed by the mighty.

**6.116.17**  And the adornment of Sītā was carried out by all the wives of Daśaratha themselves, those high-minded women making it charming.

**6.116.18**  Then the joyful Kausalyā, devoted to her son, took pains to adorn beautifully all the wives of Rāghava.

**6.116.19**  Then, at Śatrughna's command, the charioteer named Sumantra, having yoked the horses, brought forward a chariot beautiful in all its parts.

**6.116.20**  Seeing the divine chariot standing there, resembling the orb of the sun, the mighty-armed Rāma of true valor mounted it.

**6.116.21**  The ministers of King Daśaratha who were in Ayodhyā, placing the family priest at their head, took counsel purposefully:

**6.116.22**  "Deliberating for the prosperity of Rāma and for the welfare of the city, everything for the sake of the coronation — you should perform for the great-souled Rāma, who deserves victory, whatever is auspicious, preceded by sacred rites."

**6.116.23**  Thus all those ministers, having instructed the family priest, quickly went forth from the city with the intention of seeing Rāma.

**6.116.24**  Like the thousand-eyed Indra seated in a horse-drawn chariot — sinless Rāma, ascending the chariot, set out for the royal city.

**6.116.25**  Bharata took the reins; Śatrughna held the parasol; Lakṣmaṇa fanned him from above with a fan over his head.

**6.116.26**  And the white yak-tail fan was held by Sugrīva, the lord of monkeys; the other, resembling the moon, was held by Vibhīṣaṇa, the lord of Rākṣasas.

**6.116.27**  As Rāma was being praised by assemblies of sages in the sky, and by the gods together with the Marut hosts, a sweet sound was heard.

**6.116.28**  Then the greatly splendored Sugrīva, the lord of monkeys, mounted an elephant named Śatruṃjaya, which was mountain-like in size.

**6.116.29**  Nine thousand elephants set out, mounted by monkeys who had taken on human form, adorned with all manner of ornaments.

**6.116.30**  To the resounding blasts of conches and the beating of kettledrums, that tiger among men proceeded to that city garlanded with mansions.

**6.116.31**  They then saw Rāghava coming with his vanguard, shining in his form upon the chariot — that greatest of chariot-warriors.

**6.116.32**  They, having acclaimed the descendant of Kākutstha and been welcomed in return by Rāma, followed the great-souled one who was surrounded by his brothers.

**6.116.33**  Surrounded by ministers, brāhmaṇas, and the people, Rāma shone with royal glory like the moon among the stars.

**6.116.34**  He went forth accompanied by the vanguard musicians, by joyful attendants bearing tāla-palms and auspicious svastika emblems, who proclaimed blessings.

**6.116.35**  Unbroken grain, gold, cows, maidens, brāhmaṇas, and men bearing sweet cakes went ahead of Rāma.

**6.116.36**  Then Rāma recounted to the ministers his friendship with Sugrīva, the might of the son of the wind, and the deeds of the monkeys; the inhabitants of the city of Ayodhyā, hearing this, were filled with wonder.

**6.116.37**  The resplendent Rāma, having thus narrated this, surrounded by the monkeys, entered Ayodhyā, which was thronged with joyful and prosperous people.

**6.116.38**  Then the citizens raised flags on each and every house, and he reached the beautiful paternal home of the Ikṣvāku dynasty.

**6.116.39**  Reaching and entering the dwelling of his great-souled father, he saluted Kausalyā, Sumitrā, and Kaikeyī.

**6.116.40**  Then the prince, the joy of the Raghu line, spoke to Bharata, the best among the righteous, with a well-measured, sweet voice:

**6.116.41**  "Show to Sugrīva my finest dwelling, with its great grove of aśoka trees, interspersed with pearls and vaidūrya gems."

**6.116.42**  Hearing those words, the truly valorous Bharata, taking Sugrīva by the hand, entered that dwelling.

**6.116.43**  Then, prompted by Śatrughna, they quickly entered, bringing oil lamps and fine coverlets for couches.

**6.116.44**  And the greatly splendored younger brother of Rāghava said to Sugrīva: "O lord, dispatch messengers for the coronation of Rāma."

**6.116.45**  Sugrīva quickly gave four golden jars to four lords among the monkeys, adorned with all kinds of jewels.

**6.116.46**  "At the hour of dawn, wait with jars filled with the waters of the four oceans — do it thus, O monkeys."

**6.116.47**  Thus addressed, those great-souled monkeys, elephant-like in strength, leapt swiftly into the sky like fast-flying Garuḍas.

**6.116.48**  Jāmbavān, Hanūmān, the monkey Vegadarśī, and Ṛṣabha brought back water-filled jars; with pots they brought the waters of five hundred rivers.

**6.116.49**  The valiant Suṣeṇa brought a water-filled jar from the eastern ocean, adorned with all kinds of jewels.

**6.116.50**  Ṛṣabha swiftly fetched water from the southern ocean.

**6.116.51**  Gavaya brought a golden jar encased in red sandalwood and camphor, carrying water from the vast western ocean.

**6.116.52**  Mārutavikrama, swift as Garuḍa and the wind, quickly fetched cool water from the northern ocean in a great jeweled jar.

**6.116.53**  Śatrughna, together with the ministers, presented these for the coronation of Rāma to the foremost family priest and to his friends.

**6.116.54**  Then the venerable Vasiṣṭha, ritually pure, together with the brāhmaṇas, seated Rāma together with Sītā on a jeweled throne.

**6.116.55**  Vasiṣṭha, Vāmadeva, Jābāli, Kāśyapa, Kātyāyana, Suyajña, Gautama, and Vijaya —

**6.116.56**  — these consecrated that tiger among men with clear, fragrant water, as the Vasus consecrate the thousand-eyed Indra.

**6.116.57**  First the officiating priests, then brāhmaṇas, then maidens, ministers, warriors, and merchants, all filled with joy, consecrated him.

**6.116.58**  With the essences of all medicinal herbs, and also by the gods stationed in the sky, by the four guardians of the world, and by all the gods assembled together.

**6.116.59**  Śatrughna held the pure white, auspicious royal parasol over him; the white yak-tail fan was held by Sugrīva the lord of monkeys; the other, resembling the moon, was held by Vibhīṣaṇa the lord of Rākṣasas.

**6.116.60**  Vāyu, the wind god, prompted by Vāsava, gave to Rāghava a golden garland of a hundred lotuses blazing with beauty.

**6.116.61**  The one prompted by Śakra gave to the lord of men a pearl necklace filled with all kinds of precious stones and adorned with jeweled gems.

**6.116.62**  The divine Gandharvas sang and hosts of Apsarases danced at the coronation of the wise Rāma, who was worthy of such honor.

**6.116.63**  The earth brought forth abundant grain, the trees bore fruit, and the flowers became fragrant at the festival of Rāghava's coronation.

**6.116.64**  That bull among men first gave one hundred thousand horses and one hundred thousand milk cows, and one hundred bulls, to the twice-born.

**6.116.65**  Rāghava further gave to the brāhmaṇas thirty crores of gold, and various most excellent ornaments and garments.

**6.116.66**  That bull among men gave to Sugrīva a divine golden garland set with gems, resembling the rays of the sun.

**6.116.67**  The steadfast Rāma gave to Aṅgada, the son of Vālin, golden armlets embellished with vaidūrya gems and adorned with diamond gems.

**6.116.68**  Rāma gave to Sītā a peerless pearl necklace adorned with the finest gems, brilliant as the rays of the moon.

**6.116.69**  Vaidehī, gazing about, gave to the son of the wind god two spotless divine garments and auspicious ornaments.

**6.116.70**  The daughter of Janaka, removing a necklace from her own throat, looked again and again at all the monkeys and then at her husband.

**6.116.71**  Rāma, who understood her gestures, looking at her, said to the daughter of Janaka: "Give the necklace, O beautiful one, to him with whom you are pleased, O passionate lady."

**6.116.72**  She, the dark-eyed one, gave that necklace to the son of the wind god, in whom such manly virtue, valor, and wisdom are ever present.

**6.116.73**  Hanūmān, that bull among monkeys, shone with that necklace, white as the beams of the moon, like a mountain covered with white clouds.

**6.116.74**  Then the scorcher of foes, the lord of the earth, surveying all their merits and desires, gave to Dvivida, Mainda, and Nīla all that they desired.

**6.116.75**  All the elder monkeys and whatever other lords among the monkeys there were — all were honored fittingly with garments and ornaments.

**6.116.76**  All of them, honored fittingly with desired gifts and fine jewels, their hearts filled with joy, departed even as they had come.

**6.116.77**  The supremely generous Rāghava ruled with great joy; Rāma, devoted to righteousness and knowing righteousness, said to Lakṣmaṇa:

**6.116.78**  "O knower of righteousness, undertake together with me the governance of this earth, which was formerly held by royal ancestors equal in strength to me; bear the burden of the crown prince's office."

**6.116.79**  When the son of Sumitrā, though entreated with his whole being and though appointed to the crown prince's office on earth, did not accept the role, the great-souled one then consecrated Bharata.

**6.116.80**  And the righteous Rāghava, having attained the incomparable kingdom, performed sacrifices of many kinds together with friends, brothers, and kinsmen.

**6.116.81**  That bull among kings sacrificed with the Pauṇḍarīka and the Aśvamedha, repeatedly with the Vājapeya, and with many other various sacrifices.

**6.116.82**  Having ruled the kingdom for ten thousand years, Rāghava performed a hundred horse-sacrifices with fine horses and great sacrificial gifts.

**6.116.83**  Rāma, with arms hanging down to his knees, with a mighty chest, resplendent in glory, followed by Lakṣmaṇa, protected the earth.

**6.116.84**  No widow lamented, there was no fear caused by serpents, no fear born of disease either, while Rāma was governing the kingdom.

**6.116.85**  The world was free of robbers; no misfortune touched anyone; nor did the old perform the funeral rites of the young.

**6.116.86**  Everything was joyful; everyone was devoted to righteousness; keeping their eyes on Rāma alone, they did not harm one another.

**6.116.87**  People lived for a thousand years and had a thousand sons, free from disease and grief, while Rāma was governing the kingdom.

**6.116.88**  The trees bore flowers always and fruit always, with wide-spreading trunks; the rain fell in due season; the wind was pleasant to the touch.

**6.116.89**  The subjects pursued their own duties, content with their own occupations; the people were devoted to righteousness and were truthful while Rāma ruled.

**6.116.90**  All were endowed with auspicious marks; all were devoted to righteousness; Rāma caused the kingdom to flourish for ten thousand years.
